point. This phenomenon is known as gravitational redshift of light. Equation
(
261
) also shows that in our theory there are no black holes. Indeed
for the gravitational field created by a point mass
M
:
\phi=-GM/c^{2}r
.
Therefore if a photon is emitted at a distance
r
from the mass
M
with
frequency
\omega
then an observer at infinity will detect the photon with
the energy

\hbar\omega_{0}=\hbar\omega e^{-GM/c^{2}r}\text{.}

(262)

According to Eq. (
262
) no matter how close the photon is emitted to
the mass
M
the photon’s energy at infinity never becomes zero. This means
that photon can escape from the mass
M
from any distance. Such a
conclusion is dramatically different from prediction of general relativity.
In Einstein’s theory photons become trapped by the mass
M
if they are
emitted from a distance smaller then the event horizon (that is point mass
M
behaves as a black hole).

Appendix F
Equations for metric in post-Newtonian limit

Here we show that Einstein equations

R_{ik}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(T_{ik}-\frac{1}{2}g_{ik}T\right)

(263)

and equations of the vector theory of gravity (
22
) are the same in
the post-Newtonian limit. In such limit, components of the Ricci tensor are

R_{00}=\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}\left(\frac{\partial h_{0}^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial h_{\alpha}^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{0}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}+\frac{1}{2}h^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}

-\frac{1}{4}\left(\nabla h_{00}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\left(2\frac{\partial h_{\beta}^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}-\frac{\partial h_{\alpha}^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\right),

(264)

R_{0\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\beta}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0}^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{\beta}^{\beta}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{0\alpha},

(265)

where
h_{\alpha}^{\beta}=\eta^{\beta\gamma}h_{\gamma\alpha}
. Taking
into account Eq. (
31
) we obtain

R_{00}=\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}h_{00}\Delta h_{00}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla h_{00}\right)^{2},

R_{0\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{0\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\alpha}}.

As a result, Einstein equations in the post-Newtonian limit read

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}h_{00}\Delta h_{00}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla h_{00}\right)^{2}

=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(T_{00}-\frac{1}{2}g_{00}T\right),

(266)

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{0\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\alpha}}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{0\alpha}.

(267)

On the other hand in the cosmological reference frame for small deviations
of
\phi
from a constant value
\phi_{0}
(
\delta\phi=\phi-\phi_{0}
) and
|u_{\alpha}|\ll 1
, keeping post-Newtonian terms, and taking into
account that

T^{00}=\tilde{f}^{00}\tilde{f}^{00}T_{00}=e^{-4\phi}T_{00},\quad\tilde{f}^{00}=e^{-4\phi}f_{00},

T^{\alpha 0}=-T_{\alpha 0},

Eqs. (
22
) of the vector theory of gravity yield

\Delta\phi+3e^{-4\phi_{0}}\frac{\partial^{2}\phi}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-2e^{-2\phi_{0}}\cosh(2\phi_{0})\frac{\partial^{2}u^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\beta}\partial x^{0}}

=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}e^{-4\phi}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}f_{00}\right),

(268)

e^{2\phi_{0}}\cosh(2\phi_{0})\left(\frac{\partial^{2}u^{\beta}}{\partial x_{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}-\Delta u^{\alpha}\right)-2\frac{\partial^{2}\phi}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{0}}=-\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{\alpha 0}.

(269)

Next we rescale coordinates as

x^{0}\rightarrow e^{-\phi_{0}}x^{0},\quad x^{\alpha}\rightarrow e^{\phi_{0}}x^{\alpha}.

In new coordinates the equivalent metric
f_{ik}
has the form of Eq. (
32
) with

h_{00}=2\delta\phi+2(\delta\phi)^{2},\quad h_{0\alpha}=2\cosh(2\phi_{0})u_{\alpha},

\delta\phi=\frac{h_{00}}{2}-\frac{h_{00}^{2}}{4}.

In the rescaled coordinates Eqs. (
268
) and (
269
) reduce to

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}-\frac{1}{4}\Delta h_{00}^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\beta}\partial x^{0}}

=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}e^{4(\phi_{0}-\phi)}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}f_{00}\right),

(270)

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{0\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{0}}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{\alpha 0}.

(271)

Multiplying both sides of Eq. (
270
) by
e^{-4(\phi_{0}-\phi)}=e^{2h_{00}}
and expanding the exponential factor we obtain

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}+h_{00}\Delta h_{00}-\frac{1}{4}\Delta h_{00}^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\beta}\partial x^{0}}

=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}f_{00}\right).

Using

\Delta h_{00}^{2}=2(\nabla h_{00})^{2}+2h_{00}\Delta h_{00}

we finally find

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\beta}\partial x^{0}}+\frac{1}{2}h_{00}\Delta h_{00}-\frac{1}{2}(\nabla h_{00})^{2}

=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}f_{00}\right).

(272)

Equations (
272
) and (
271
) of the vector theory of gravity are
identical to the Einstein equations (
266
) and (
267
). Boundary
conditions are also the same. Thus, in the post-Newtonian limit both
theories are equivalent.

Appendix G
Analogy of weak gravity in the classical limit with
electromagnetism in medium with negative refractive index

In a medium with dielectric constant
\varepsilon
and magnetic
permeability
\mu
Maxwell equations describing electromagnetic field read

\text{curl}\mathbf{E}=-\frac{1}{c}\frac{\partial\mathbf{B}}{\partial t},

(273)

\text{div}(\varepsilon\mathbf{E})=4\pi\rho_{e},

(274)

\text{curl}\left(\frac{\mathbf{B}}{\mu}\right)=\frac{1}{c}\frac{\partial(\varepsilon\mathbf{E})}{\partial t}+\frac{4\pi}{c}\rho_{e}\mathbf{V},

(275)

where
\rho_{e}
is the electric charge density. In terms of the vector
\mathbf{A}
and scalar
\varphi
potentials

\mathbf{E}=-\nabla\varphi-\frac{\partial\mathbf{A}}{\partial x^{0}},\quad\mathbf{B=}\text{curl}\mathbf{(A})\text{,}

the Maxwell equations (
273
)-(
275
) are

\Delta\varphi+\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}\text{div}\mathbf{A}=-\frac{4\pi}{\varepsilon}\rho_{e},

\left(\Delta-\varepsilon\mu\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}\right)\mathbf{A}-\nabla\left(\varepsilon\mu\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x^{0}}+\text{div}\mathbf{A}\right)=-\frac{4\pi\mu}{c}\rho_{e}\mathbf{V}.

In the Lorenz gauge

\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x^{0}}+\text{div}\mathbf{A}=0

(276)

and for
\varepsilon=\mu=-1
equations reduce to

\left(\Delta-\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}\right)\varphi=4\pi\rho_{e},

(277)

\left(\Delta\mathbf{-}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}\right)\mathbf{A}=\frac{4\pi}{c}\rho_{e}\mathbf{V}.

(278)

On the other hand, equations for the weak classical gravitational field and
non relativistic motion of matter with density
\rho_{\text{m}}
and
velocity
\mathbf{V}
are [see Eqs. (
38
) and (
39
)]

\left(\Delta+3\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}\right)h_{00}-2\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\beta}}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{2}}\rho_{\text{m}},

(279)

\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-\Delta\right)\mathbf{h}+2\nabla\left(\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\right)=-\frac{16\pi G}{c^{3}}\rho_{\text{m}}\mathbf{V},

(280)

where
\mathbf{h}=h^{0\alpha}
,
\mathbf{V}=V^{\alpha}
and
\nabla=\partial/\partial x^{\alpha}
. Introducing

\tilde{\varphi}=\frac{c^{2}}{2}h_{00},\quad\mathbf{\tilde{A}}=\frac{c^{2}}{4}\mathbf{h}

we obtain

\left(\Delta+3\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}\right)\tilde{\varphi}+4\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}\text{div}\mathbf{\tilde{A}}=4\pi G\rho_{\text{m}},

(281)

\left(\Delta-\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{A}}-\nabla\left(\frac{\partial\tilde{\varphi}}{\partial x^{0}}+\text{div}\mathbf{\tilde{A}}\right)=\frac{4\pi G}{c}\rho_{\text{m}}\mathbf{V}.

(282)

Taking
\partial/\partial x^{0}
from Eq. (
281
) and
(1/2)
div from
Eq. (
282
), adding these equations together and using the continuity
equation

\frac{\partial\rho}{\partial t}+\text{div}\left(\rho_{\text{m}}\mathbf{V}\right)=0

we find

\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}\left(\frac{\partial\tilde{\varphi}}{\partial x^{0}}+\text{div}\mathbf{\tilde{A}}\right)=0.

Therefore one can take

\frac{\partial\tilde{\varphi}}{\partial x^{0}}+\text{div}\mathbf{\tilde{A}}=0

which is the same equation as the Lorenz gauge condition (
276
) in
electromagnetism. Then Eqs. (
281
) and (
282
) reduce to

\left(\Delta-\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}\right)\tilde{\varphi}=4\pi G\rho_{\text{m}},

(283)

\left(\Delta-\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{A}}=\frac{4\pi G}{c}\rho_{\text{m}}\mathbf{V},

(284)

which have the same form as Maxwell equations (
277
) and (
278
) in
the left handed medium with
\varepsilon=\mu=-1
.

The analogy becomes more transparent if we compare expressions for the
energy density and energy flux. According to Eqs. (
46
) and (
47
), the energy density and the energy density flux of the transverse
gravitational wave in terms of
\tilde{\varphi}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{A}}
are

w_{\text{tr}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi G}\left[\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{\tilde{A}}}{\partial x^{0}}\right)^{2}+\text{curl}^{2}\mathbf{\tilde{A}}\right],

\mathbf{S}_{\text{tr}}\mathbf{=}\frac{c}{\pi G}\frac{\partial\mathbf{\tilde{A}}}{\partial x^{0}}\times\text{curl}\mathbf{(\tilde{A}}),

which are similar to those for a transverse (div
\mathbf{A}=0
)
electromagnetic wave in the left handed medium with
\varepsilon=\mu=-1

w_{\text{em}}=-\frac{1}{8\pi}\left[\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{A}}{\partial x^{0}}\right)^{2}+\text{curl}^{2}\mathbf{A}\right],

\mathbf{S}_{\text{em}}\mathbf{=}\frac{c}{4\pi}\frac{\partial\mathbf{A}}{\partial x^{0}}\times\text{curl}\mathbf{(A}).

In such left handed dispersionless medium the energy density of the
electromagnetic field is also negative. However, equation of mass motion in
weak gravitational field (
40
) is somewhat different from the equation
of motion of a charge in electromagnetic field.

Appendix H
Energy density and energy flux for weak classical gravitational
field

One can obtain the energy density and energy flux for classical
gravitational field using general formula for the energy-momentum tensor.
Namely, if action of the system has the form

S=\frac{1}{c}\int d^{4}xL\left(A_{l},\frac{\partial A_{l}}{\partial x^{k}}\right),

where the Lagrangian density
L
is some function of the quantities
A_{l}
,
describing the state of the system, and of their first derivatives, then the
energy-momentum tensor
T^{ik}
of the system can be calculated using
equation
Land95

T_{i}^{k}=\sum_{l}\frac{\partial A_{l}}{\partial x^{i}}\frac{\partial L}{\partial\frac{\partial A_{l}}{\partial x^{k}}}-\delta_{i}^{k}L.

(285)

T_{i}^{k}
obeys the conservation law

\frac{\partial T_{i}^{k}}{\partial x^{k}}=0

and, therefore,
T^{00}
can be interpreted as the energy density of the
system, while vector
S^{\alpha}=cT^{0\alpha}
(the Poynting vector) is
the flux density (the amount of energy passing through unit surface per unit
time).

For weak gravitational field and nonrelativistic motion of masses the
Lagrangian density reads

L=\frac{c^{4}}{32\pi G}\left(-3\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}}\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}-\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{0}}\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{0}}\right.

+\left.\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}-\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}+2\left[\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{0}}\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}+\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}}\right]\right)

-\rho c^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\rho c^{2}h_{00}-\rho cV^{\alpha}h_{0\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\rho V^{2}

(286)

and components of the equivalent metric
h_{0k}
can be treated as function
describing the state of the gravitational field. Applying Eq. (
285
) we
find

T^{00}=\frac{c^{4}}{32\pi G}\left(-3\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}}\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{0}}\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{0}}+\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\right.

-\left.\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\right)+\rho c^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\rho c^{2}h_{00}+\frac{1}{2}\rho V^{2},

(287)

T^{0\alpha}=\frac{c^{4}}{16\pi G}\left(-\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}}+\frac{\partial h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{0}}\right.

\left.+2\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{0}}\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}}\right)+\rho cV^{\alpha},

(288)

where
\rho
is the mass density,
\mathbf{V}
is the velocity of
nonrelativistic motion of matter and
\alpha=1
,
2
,
3
. Introducing
vector

\mathbf{h}=h^{{}_{0\alpha}},

we obtain the following expression for the energy density of the weak
classical gravitational field

T^{00}=-\frac{c^{4}}{32\pi G}\left[3\left(\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}}\right)^{2}-\left(\nabla h_{00}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{h}}{\partial x^{0}}\right)^{2}+\text{curl}^{2}\mathbf{h}\right]

+\rho c^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\rho c^{2}h_{00}+\frac{1}{2}\rho V^{2}.

(289)

The energy density flux is given by

\mathbf{S=}\frac{c^{5}}{16\pi G}\left[-\left(2\frac{\partial\mathbf{h}}{\partial x^{0}}+\nabla h_{00}\right)\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}}+\frac{\partial\mathbf{h}}{\partial x^{0}}\times\text{curl }\mathbf{h}\right]+\rho c\mathbf{V}.

(290)

In the Newtonian limit the energy density of field and matter reads

w=\rho c^{2}+\frac{\rho V^{2}}{2}+\rho c^{2}\phi+\frac{c^{4}}{8\pi G}(\nabla\phi)^{2},

(291)

where
c^{2}\phi=c^{2}h_{00}/2
is the Newtonian gravitational potential.

Appendix I
Cosmological suppression of preferred frame and preferred location
effects

Here we show lack of the preferred frame and preferred location effects for
neutron starts orbiting each other. Since gravitational field of a neutron
star is not weak we must find symmetries of the action valid when spatial
change of
\phi
is of the order of unity. Spatial variation of
\phi

produced by a neutron star yet substantially smaller than cosmological value

\phi_{\text{cosm}}
. Indeed, due to expansion of the Universe the spatial
scale has been magnified in a factor
e^{-\phi_{\text{cosm}}}\sim 10^{40}
.
Thus, at the present epoch
e^{-\phi}\ggg 1
, and, therefore, we can
disregard exponentially small number
e^{\phi}
compared to the
exponentially large value of
e^{-\phi}
.

In terms of components the equivalent metric (
9
) reads

f_{00}=e^{2\phi}-2\cosh(2\phi)u_{\alpha}^{2},

f_{0\alpha}=2\cosh(2\phi)u_{0}u_{\alpha},

f_{\alpha\beta}=-e^{-2\phi}\delta_{\alpha\beta}+2\cosh(2\phi)u_{\alpha}u_{\beta}.

Taking into account that
e^{-\phi}\ggg 1
and introducing new function

h_{0\alpha}=e^{-2\phi}u_{\alpha}

one can write the equivalent metric as

f_{00}=e^{2\phi}\left(1-h_{0\alpha}^{2}\right),\quad f_{0\alpha}=h_{0\alpha},\quad f_{\alpha\beta}=-e^{-2\phi}\delta_{\alpha\beta}.

(292)

Thus, the square of the interval is

ds^{2}=e^{2\phi}\left(1-h_{0\alpha}^{2}\right)(dx^{0})^{2}+2h_{0\alpha}dx^{0}dx^{\alpha}-e^{-2\phi}d\mathbf{r}^{2}.

(293)

Motion of stars orbiting each other is not relativistic and, therefore,
V/c

is another small parameter in our problem. Keeping terms upto
V^{3}/c^{3}

and taking into account that
e^{-\phi}\ggg 1
the gravitational field
action (
21
) reduces to

S_{\text{gravity}}=\frac{c^{3}}{8\pi G}\int d^{4}x\left[-\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial x^{\alpha}}-3e^{-4\phi}\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial x^{0}}\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial x^{0}}\right.

+2e^{-2\phi}\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{0}}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}-\frac{1}{4}\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}

+h_{0\beta}\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}+h_{0\alpha}\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial x^{\beta}}

-2e^{-2\phi}h_{0\alpha}\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial x^{0}}+\left.h_{0\alpha}h_{0\alpha}\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial x^{\beta}}\right].

(294)

The interval (
293
) and the gravitational field action (
294
)
are invariant under scaling transformation

x^{0}\rightarrow e^{-a}x^{0},\qquad x^{\alpha}\rightarrow e^{a}x^{\alpha},

(295)

\phi\rightarrow\phi+a,\qquad h_{0\alpha}\rightarrow h_{0\alpha},

(296)

where
a
is an arbitrary constant parameter, not necessarily small.

There is also an additional Lorentz-like symmetry of the action valid for
the strong gravitational field of a neutron star. Straightforward but
lengthy calculation yields that upto the terms of the order of
V^{3}/c^{3}

the total action
S_{\text{gravity}}+S_{\text{matter}}
is invariant under a
coordinate transformation for which derivatives transform as

\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}\rightarrow\left(1+\frac{V^{2}}{2c^{2}}\right)\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\mathbf{V}}{c}\nabla,\quad\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}\rightarrow\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}-\frac{\mathbf{V}}{c}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}+

\left(\left[1+8e^{-4\phi}-9e^{4\phi}\right]\frac{V^{2}}{2c^{2}}-\frac{e^{2\phi}}{c}\mathbf{V\cdot h}\right)\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}+

e^{4\phi}\frac{\mathbf{V}}{2c^{2}}\left(\mathbf{V}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}\right)+e^{2\phi}\frac{\mathbf{V}}{c}\left(\mathbf{h}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}\right),

(297)

where
\mathbf{V}=V^{\alpha}
is a constant (velocity) vector and
\mathbf{h}=h^{0\alpha}
. Under this transformation the equivalent metric
f_{ik}
,
given by Eq. (
292
), transforms as a covariant tensor. Namely,
f_{00}

transforms as
\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}
,
f_{0\alpha}
transforms as
\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\alpha}}
and so on. Since
f_{ik}
transforms as a
tensor the interval
ds
, and hence
S_{\text{matter}}
, are invariant.

Keeping terms of the proper order the metric transformation reads

h_{0\alpha}\rightarrow h_{0\alpha}-2\frac{V^{\alpha}}{c}\text{sinh}(2\phi),

(298)

e^{2\phi}\rightarrow e^{2\phi}+2\frac{V^{2}}{c^{2}}\text{sinh}(2\phi)-2e^{4\phi}\frac{V^{\alpha}}{c}h_{0\alpha},

or

\phi\rightarrow\phi+\frac{V^{2}}{c^{2}}e^{-2\phi}\text{sinh}(2\phi)-\frac{V^{\alpha}}{c}e^{2\phi}h_{0\alpha}.

(299)

Under transformation (
297
) the volume element
d^{4}x
in the action
S_{\text{gravity}}
transforms as
d^{4}x\rightarrow d^{4}x/J
, where
J
is
the Jacobian of the transformation

J=1+\left(1+12e^{-4\phi}-13e^{4\phi}\right)\frac{V^{2}}{c^{2}}+2e^{2\phi}\frac{V^{\alpha}}{c}h_{0\alpha}.

In the post-Newtonian limit (far away from the neutron star) the
transformation (
297
)-(
299
) reduces to the low-velocity Lorentz
transformation

x^{0}\rightarrow\left(1+\frac{V^{2}}{2c^{2}}\right)x^{0}+\frac{1}{c}\mathbf{V\cdot r},\quad\mathbf{r}\rightarrow\mathbf{r}+\frac{\mathbf{V}}{c}x^{0},

(300)

\phi\rightarrow\left(1+\frac{2V^{2}}{c^{2}}\right)\phi-\frac{V^{\alpha}}{c}h_{0\alpha},

(301)

h_{0\alpha}\rightarrow h_{0\alpha}-4\frac{V^{\alpha}}{c}\phi.

(302)

Scaling transformation (
295
) and (
296
) combined with the
Lorentz-like transformation (
297
)-(
299
) allow us to eliminate
the preferred frame and preferred location from the equations describing
motion and gravitational field of a neutron star. Indeed, let us consider a
reference frame in which background gravitational field is
\phi^{\text{back}}=
const and
h_{0\alpha}^{\text{back}}=
const. The background field is
produced by the companion star and the cosmological part. Since the total
action is invariant under rotations in the four dimensional Euclidean space
\delta_{ik}
one can eliminate
h_{0\alpha}^{\text{back}}
by making such
a rotation. After this transformation the background field becomes
\phi^{\text{back}}=\phi_{0}=
const and
h_{0\alpha}^{\text{back}}=0
. Next we
perform scaling transformation (
295
) and (
296
) with
a=-\phi_{0}
which makes
\phi^{\text{back}}=0
, that is now the background
metric is Minkowski metric
\eta_{ik}
. In the new frame, however, the
neutron star moves with some velocity
\mathbf{V}
. Finally, the
Lorentz-like transformation (
297
)-(
299
) eliminates
\mathbf{V}
,
that is in the new reference frame the neutron star is at rest. At the same
time, the Lorentz-like transformation does not change the background
Minkowski metric
\eta_{ik}
. Indeed, far from the star the field
transformation reduces to Eqs. (
301
) and (
302
) for which the
asymptotic values
\phi=0
and
h_{0\alpha}=0
remain invariant.

We found that transformations which keep the total action invariant
eliminate the background field from the boundary conditions. Thus, field
equations and the equation of the star motion do not yield preferred frame
and preferred location effects for they are obtained by taking variation of
the total action. As a consequence, one can choose a reference frame in
which star is static and metric is asymptotically Minkowski. In this frame,
in the outer region of a nonrotating static star the gravitational field is
described by the equation
\Delta\phi=0
with asymptotic boundary
conditions
\phi^{\text{back}}=0
and
h_{0\alpha}^{\text{back}}=0
which
yields

\phi(\mathbf{r})=-\frac{GM}{c^{2}r},

(303)

where
M
is a Kepler-measured mass of the star. Thus, solution for the
relativistic structure and gravitational field of the star is independent of
the background gravitational field.

To obtain gravitational field in a frame in which star moves with velocity
V\ll c
one can make Lorentz-like transformation (
297
)-(
299
)
which yields analytical solution for arbitrary values of
\phi
. Using Eqs.
(
292
) we find the following expression for the equivalent metric
produced by the moving star

f_{00}=e^{2\phi}+2\frac{V^{2}}{c^{2}}\text{sinh}(2\phi)\left(2-e^{4\phi}\right),

(304)

f_{0\alpha}=-2\frac{V^{\alpha}}{c}\text{sinh}(2\phi),

(305)

f_{\alpha\beta}=\left(-e^{-2\phi}+2e^{-4\phi}\frac{V^{2}}{c^{2}}\text{sinh}(2\phi)\right)\delta_{\alpha\beta},

(306)

where
\phi
is the field of the static star written in terms of
coordinates in the moving frame. In the far field in the post-Newtonian
limit Eqs (
304
)-(
306
) reduce to

h_{00}=-\frac{2GM}{c^{2}|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{V}t|}\left(1+\frac{2V^{2}}{c^{2}}\right)+\frac{2G^{2}M^{2}}{c^{4}|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{V}t|^{2}},

(307)

h_{0\alpha}=\frac{4G}{c^{3}}\frac{MV^{\alpha}}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{V}t|},

(308)

h_{\alpha\beta}=-\frac{2GM}{c^{2}|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{V}t|}\delta_{\alpha\beta}.

(309)

Our result coincides with those obtained in general relativity (in the gauge

2\partial h_{00}/\partial x^{0}-\partial h_{0\beta}/\partial x^{\beta}=0
) in the post-Newtonian limit far from the star. It is independent of the
original background metric as well as motion of the reference frame relative
to the background.

Appendix J
Post-Newtonian limit of vector gravity in the parametrized
post-Newtonian formalism

Clifford Will in his book on
“Theory and experiment
in gravitational physics”
Will93
presented a
method of calculation of the post-Newtonian (PN) limits of any metric theory
of gravity. Vector gravity is a metric theory and, thus, approach of Ref.

Will93
can be applied here as well.

The method outlined in the Will’s book consists of
9
steps. Namely, one
should start from the basic field equations of a metric theory of gravity,
solve them in the PN limit for the equivalent metric and compare the answer
with the parametrized post-Newtonian (PPN) expansion. In Appendix
F

we carried out the first four steps and found that equations of vector
gravity in the PN limit are identical to those of general relativity, so do
the boundary conditions. If this is the case then equivalence of vector
gravity and general relativity in the PN limit is proven. Calculations of
steps
5-9
dealing with the actual solution of the equations are simply
identical to those in general relativity.

However, one of the referees of our paper believes that calculations of
Appendix
F
are not sufficient and all
9
steps must be included. We
present them here closely following prescription of Ref.
Will93
. For
completeness of the presentation we repeat the first four steps as well.

In the PN formalism the metric is expanded in a small parameter
\epsilon
.
The “order of smallness” is determined
according to the rules that matter velocity is of order
V\sim\epsilon^{1/2}
and gravitational constant
G\sim\epsilon
. A consistent PN limit
requires determination of
g_{00}
correction through
O(\epsilon^{2})
,
g_{0\alpha}
through
O(\epsilon^{3/2})
, and
g_{\alpha\beta}
through
O(\epsilon)
.

Recall, that we use the following convention. Lower case Latin indices (
i
,

k
,
m
, …) label four dimensional coordinates (range
0
,
1
,
2
,
3
), while lower case Greek letters
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\gamma
denote
spatial coordinates (range
1
,
2
,
3
).

Step 1.

Identify the variables.

In vector gravity the scalar
\phi
and the unit vector
u_{k}
are
dynamical gravitational variables and flat background metric
\delta_{ik}

is the prior-geometrical variable.

Step 2.
Set the cosmological boundary conditions. Assume a
homogeneous isotropic cosmology, and at a chosen moment of time define the
values of the variables far from the PN system. Rest frame of the universe
is a convenient choice of the coordinate system.

Please note that PN expansion of a metric theory of gravity and comparison
with general relativity can be made in any convenient reference frame. This
is true for any metric theory of gravity, including vector gravity. This
question is explained well in
Will93
.

For vector gravity, the cosmological boundary conditions are:

\phi\rightarrow\phi_{0},\quad u_{k}\rightarrow(1,0,0,0)

far from the PN system.

Step 3.

Expand in a post-Newtonian series about the
asymptotic values.

Expansion of the dynamical gravitational variables in vector gravity is

\phi=\phi_{0}+\tilde{\phi},\quad u_{k}=(1,0,0,0)+\tilde{u}_{k},

where
|\tilde{\phi}|,|\tilde{u}_{k}|\ll 1
.

Step 4.

Substitute these forms into the field equations,
keeping only such terms as are necessary to obtain a final, consistent PN
solution for
h_{ik}
.

Keeping the post-Newtonian terms, and taking into account that

T^{00}=\tilde{f}^{00}\tilde{f}^{00}T_{00}=e^{-4\phi}T_{00},\quad\tilde{f}^{00}=e^{-4\phi}f_{00},

T^{\alpha 0}=-T_{\alpha 0},

the basic field equations of vector gravity (
22
) reduce to the
following equations for
\tilde{\phi}
and
\tilde{u}_{k}

\Delta\tilde{\phi}+3e^{-4\phi_{0}}\frac{\partial^{2}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-2e^{-2\phi_{0}}\cosh(2\phi_{0})\frac{\partial^{2}\tilde{u}^{\beta}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\beta}}

=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}e^{-4(\phi_{0}+\tilde{\phi})}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}f_{00}\right),

(310)

e^{2\phi_{0}}\cosh(2\phi_{0})\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\tilde{u}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}-\Delta\tilde{u}^{\alpha}\right)-2\frac{\partial^{2}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{0}}=-\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{0\alpha}.

(311)

Next we rescale coordinates as

x^{0}\rightarrow e^{-\phi_{0}}x^{0},\quad x^{\alpha}\rightarrow e^{\phi_{0}}x^{\alpha}.

In the new coordinates the equivalent metric
f_{ik}
has the form

f_{ik}=\eta_{ik}+\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}h_{00}&h_{01}&h_{02}&h_{03}\\
h_{01}&h_{00}&0&0\\
h_{02}&0&h_{00}&0\\
h_{03}&0&0&h_{00}\end{array}\right),

(312)

where
\eta_{ik}=
diag
(1,-1,-1,-1)
is Minkowski metric and

h_{00}=2\tilde{\phi}+2\tilde{\phi}^{2},\quad h_{0\alpha}=2\cosh(2\phi_{0})\tilde{u}_{\alpha},

\tilde{\phi}=\frac{h_{00}}{2}-\frac{h_{00}^{2}}{4}.

In the rescaled coordinates in terms of
h_{00}
and
h_{0\alpha}
Eqs. (
310
) and (
311
) read

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}-\frac{1}{4}\Delta h_{00}^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\beta}}

=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}e^{-4\tilde{\phi}}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}f_{00}\right),

(313)

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{0\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{0}}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{0\alpha}.

(314)

Multiplying both sides of Eq. (
313
) by
e^{4\tilde{\phi}}\approx e^{2h_{00}}
and expanding the exponential factor we obtain

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}+h_{00}\Delta h_{00}-\frac{1}{4}\Delta h_{00}^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\beta}\partial x^{0}}

=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}f_{00}\right).

Using

\Delta h_{00}^{2}=2(\nabla h_{00})^{2}+2h_{00}\Delta h_{00}

we find

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}h_{00}\Delta h_{00}-\frac{1}{2}(\nabla h_{00})^{2}

=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}f_{00}\right).

(315)

Equations (
314
), (
315
) of vector gravity are identical to the
Einstein equations

R_{ik}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(T_{ik}-\frac{1}{2}g_{ik}T\right)

(316)

in the PN limit. Indeed, let us consider small deviations
h_{ik}
of the
tensor gravitational field
g_{ik}
from the Minkowski metric
\eta_{ik}

g_{ik}=\eta_{ik}+h_{ik}.

In the PN limit, components of the Ricci tensor are

R_{00}=\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}\left(\frac{\partial h_{0}^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial h_{\alpha}^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{0}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}+\frac{1}{2}h^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}

-\frac{1}{4}\left(\nabla h_{00}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\left(2\frac{\partial h_{\beta}^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}-\frac{\partial h_{\alpha}^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\right),

(317)

R_{0\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\beta}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0}^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{\beta}^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{0}}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{0\alpha},

(318)

R_{\alpha\beta}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{\alpha}^{m}}{\partial x^{\beta}\partial x^{m}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{\beta}^{m}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{m}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{m}^{m}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{\alpha\beta},

(319)

where
h_{\alpha}^{\beta}=\eta^{\beta\gamma}h_{\gamma\alpha}
. If we
impose the three gauge conditions (
\gamma=1,2,3
)

\frac{\partial h_{\gamma}^{m}}{\partial x^{m}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial h_{m}^{m}}{\partial x^{\gamma}}=0

(320)

equation (
319
) becomes

R_{\alpha\beta}=\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{\alpha\beta}

and, in the order
O(\epsilon)
, Einstein equations (
316
) with
ik=\alpha\beta
reduce to

\Delta h_{\alpha\beta}=-\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T\eta_{\alpha\beta}.

(321)

On the other hand, in this order, Einstein equations with
ik=00
yield

\Delta h_{00}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(2T_{00}-T\right)=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T.

(322)

Comparing Eqs. (
321
) and (
322
) we find that in the PN limit of
general relativity

h_{\beta}^{\alpha}=-h_{00}\delta_{\beta}^{\alpha}

(323)

and, hence, the metric is given by

g_{ik}=\eta_{ik}+\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}h_{00}&h_{01}&h_{02}&h_{03}\\
h_{01}&h_{00}&0&0\\
h_{02}&0&h_{00}&0\\
h_{03}&0&0&h_{00}\end{array}\right).

(324)

Metric (
324
) has the same form as the equivalent metric (
312
)
in vector gravity. Plugging Eq. (
323
) into Eqs. (
317
) and (
318
) we obtain

R_{00}=\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}h_{00}\Delta h_{00}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla h_{00}\right)^{2},

R_{0\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{0\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{0}}.

As a result, Einstein equations (
316
) with
i=0
and
k=0,1,2,3
in
the PN limit read

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{0}}-\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{0}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}h_{00}\Delta h_{00}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla h_{00}\right)^{2}

=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}g_{00}\right),

(325)

\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{0\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{0\beta}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{\beta}}+\frac{\partial^{2}h_{00}}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial x^{0}}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{0\alpha}.

(326)

Equations (
325
) and (
326
) for the four unknown functions
h_{00}
,
h_{0\alpha}
coincide with Eqs. (
315
) and (
314
) of vector
gravity. Boundary conditions are also the same in both theories, namely, far
from the PN system

h_{00}\rightarrow 0,\quad h_{0\alpha}\rightarrow 0.

This is sufficient to conclude that vector gravity and general relativity
are equivalent in the PN limit.

Step 5.
Solve for
h_{00}
to
O(\epsilon)
.

Only the lowest PN order equation is needed. In this order Eq. (
315
)
of vector gravity reduces to

\Delta h_{00}=\frac{16\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}\right)=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{2}}\rho,

(327)

where
\rho(t,\mathbf{r})
is the matter density (measured in a frame
momentarily comoving with the matter). Using

\Delta\frac{1}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}=-4\pi\delta(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime})

we obtain that solution of Eq. (
327
) is

h_{00}=-\frac{2}{c^{2}}U,

(328)

where

U(t,\mathbf{r})=G\int\frac{\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}

is the Newtonian gravitational potential with minus sign.

Step 6.
Solve for
h_{\alpha\beta}
to
O(\epsilon)
and
h_{0\alpha}
to
O(\epsilon^{3/2}).

According to Eqs. (
312
) and (
328
), in vector gravity

h_{\alpha\beta}=h_{00}\delta_{\alpha\beta}=-\frac{2}{c^{2}}U\delta_{\alpha\beta}\text{.}

Next we note that with the PN accuracy Eqs. (
314
), (
315
) are
invariant under the gauge transformation

h_{00}\rightarrow h_{00}+2\frac{\partial\psi}{\partial x^{0}},\quad h_{0\alpha}\rightarrow h_{0\alpha}+\frac{\partial\psi}{\partial x^{\alpha}},

where
\psi
is an arbitrary function of the order of
O(\epsilon)
. Thus,
we can impose one gauge fixing condition which we choose as in Refs.
Will93
;
Land95

\frac{\partial h_{0}^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial h_{\alpha}^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{0}}=0

or

\frac{\partial h_{0\alpha}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}=\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial h_{00}}{\partial x^{0}}.

(329)

Then, using Eq. (
328
), Eq. (
314
) reduces to

\Delta h_{0\alpha}-\frac{1}{c^{3}}\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial t}=\frac{16\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{0\alpha},

(330)

where with the required accuracy

T_{0\alpha}=\rho cV_{\alpha}

and
V_{\alpha}=dx_{\alpha}/dt
is the velocity of matter.

Solution of Eq. (
330
) satisfying the proper boundary condition is

h_{0\alpha}=-\frac{4G}{c^{3}}\int\frac{\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})V_{\alpha}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{c^{3}}\frac{\partial^{2}F}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial t},

(331)

where
F
is the solution of the auxiliary equation

\Delta F=U=G\int\frac{\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}.

Using the relation
\Delta r=2/r
, we find

F(t,\mathbf{r})=\frac{G}{2}\int\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}.

Applying the continuity equation

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\rho(t,\mathbf{r})+\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}(\rho\mathbf{V})=0,

where
\mathbf{V}\equiv V^{\alpha}
, we obtain

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}F(t,\mathbf{r})=\frac{G}{2}\int d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})=

=-\frac{G}{2}\int d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}\left[\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\mathbf{V}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\right]=

=\frac{G}{2}\int d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\mathbf{V}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|=

=-\frac{G}{2}\int d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\frac{\mathbf{V}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\cdot(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}.

Taking derivative with respect to
x^{\alpha}
we have

\frac{\partial^{2}F(t,\mathbf{r})}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial t}=-\frac{G}{2}\int d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}\frac{\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})V^{\alpha}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}+

+\frac{G}{2}\int d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\frac{\left[\mathbf{V}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\cdot(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\right](x^{\alpha}-x^{\prime\alpha})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|^{3}}.

Substituting this into Eq. (
331
) and taking into account that
V^{\alpha}=-V_{\alpha}
,
x^{\alpha}=-x_{\alpha}
we finally find

h_{0\alpha}=-\frac{7G}{2c^{3}}\int\frac{\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})V_{\alpha}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}-

-\frac{G}{2c^{3}}\int\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\frac{\left[\mathbf{V}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\cdot(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\right](x_{\alpha}-x_{\alpha}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|^{3}}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}.

Step 7.
Solve for
h_{00}
to
O(\epsilon^{2})
.

In the gauge (
329
), keeping terms of the
O(\epsilon^{2})
order, Eq. (
315
) reduces to

\Delta h_{00}+h_{00}\Delta h_{00}-(\nabla h_{00})^{2}=\frac{16\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}f_{00}\right).

Using

(\nabla h_{00})^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\Delta h_{00}^{2}-h_{00}\Delta h_{00}

we obtain

\Delta\left(h_{00}-\frac{1}{2}h_{00}^{2}\right)+2h_{00}\Delta h_{00}=\frac{16\pi G}{c^{4}}\left(T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}f_{00}\right).

Substituting in the higher-order terms the known lower-order solution
h_{00}=-2U/c^{2}
,
\Delta h_{00}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{2}}\rho
and taking into
account that
f_{00}\approx 1+h_{00}
, we get

\Delta\left(h_{00}-\frac{2}{c^{4}}U^{2}\right)=

\frac{16\pi G}{c^{4}}\left[T_{00}-\frac{T}{2}\left(1-\frac{2U}{c^{2}}\right)+2\rho U\right].

(332)

We will use a perfect fluid as a model of matter. Then in curved space-time
with the equivalent metric
f_{ik}
the energy-momentum tensor of matter
reads

T_{ik}=\left(\varepsilon+P\right)v_{i}v_{k}-Pf_{ik},

where
\varepsilon=\rho c^{2}(1+\Pi)
is the rest energy density of the
fluid,
\Pi
is the specific density of thermal energy,
P
is the
isotropic pressure and
v^{k}=dx^{k}/ds
is the four-velocity of the fluid
element. Here
ds=\sqrt{f_{ik}dx^{i}dx^{k}}
and
v_{i}=f_{ik}v^{k}
. Trace
of
T_{ik}
is
T=\varepsilon-3P
.

With the required accuracy
ds\approx cdt\sqrt{1+h_{00}-V^{2}/c^{2}}
and,
therefore,

v_{0}^{2}=f_{00}^{\text{ }2}\frac{c^{2}dt^{2}}{ds^{2}}\approx\frac{\left(1+h_{00}\right)^{2}}{1+h_{00}-V^{2}/c^{2}}\approx 1-\frac{2U}{c^{2}}+\frac{V^{2}}{c^{2}},

which yields

T_{00}\approx\varepsilon\left(1-\frac{2U}{c^{2}}+\frac{V^{2}}{c^{2}}\right).

Substituting this into Eq. (
332
) we obtain to the required accuracy

\Delta\left(h_{00}-\frac{2}{c^{4}}U^{2}\right)=

\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}\left[\rho c^{2}\left(1+\Pi\right)+2\rho\left(V^{2}+U\right)+3P\right].

(333)

Solution of Eq. (
333
) is

h_{00}=-\frac{2}{c^{2}}U+\frac{2}{c^{4}}U^{2}-\frac{G}{c^{4}}\int\left[4\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\left(V^{2}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})+U(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\right)\right.

+\left.2c^{2}\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\Pi(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})+6P(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\right]\frac{d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}.

Steps 8 and 9.
Equivalent metric and PPN parameters.

The final form for the equivalent metric is

f_{00}=1-\frac{2}{c^{2}}U+\frac{2}{c^{4}}U^{2}-4\Phi_{1}-4\Phi_{2}-2\Phi_{3}-6\Phi_{4},

(334)

f_{0\alpha}=-\frac{7}{2}\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}W_{\alpha},

(335)

f_{\alpha\beta}=-\left(1+\frac{2U}{c^{2}}\right)\delta_{\alpha\beta},

(336)

where

U=G\int\frac{\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\quad\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}=\frac{G}{c^{3}}\int\frac{\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})V_{\alpha}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime},

W_{\alpha}=\frac{G}{c^{3}}\int\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\frac{\left[\mathbf{V}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\cdot(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\right](x_{\alpha}-x_{\alpha}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|^{3}}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime},

\Phi_{1}=\frac{G}{c^{4}}\int\frac{\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})V^{2}(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime},

\Phi_{2}=\frac{G}{c^{4}}\int\frac{\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})U(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime},

\Phi_{3}=\frac{G}{c^{2}}\int\frac{\rho(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\Pi(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\quad\Phi_{4}=\frac{G}{c^{4}}\int\frac{P(t,\mathbf{r}^{\prime})}{|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime}|}d\mathbf{r}^{\prime}

are metric potentials defined in the same way as in Ref.
Will93
. One
should note that in
Will93
the Minkowski metric is chosen as
\eta_{ik}=
diag
(-1,1,1,1)
and, as a result, formulas for
f_{00}
and
f_{\alpha\beta}
have the opposite sign.

The metric (
334
)-(
336
) is written in the standard PPN gauge and,
hence, the PPN parameters can be read off immediately

\gamma=\beta=1,\quad\zeta=0,

\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}=\zeta_{1}=\zeta_{2}=\zeta_{3}=\zeta_{4}=0.

They are the same as in general relativity. Thus, vector gravity is a fully
conservative theory of gravity and predicts no preferred-frame effects in
the PN limit. Moreover, in Appendix
I
we show that in vector
gravity there are no preferred-frame effects in the
V^{2}/c^{2}
order in
the matter velocity for arbitrary large values of the gravitational
potential.
